- LANE C 2026-11-14T19:32:04Z: Cilium D09 guard is CORRECT, restored in 85c7f9af2. Removing the two `#ifndef MICRO_NATIVE` guards in vendor/bpf/cilium/bpf/bpf_lxc.c broke the load: run x86_kvm_corpus_20261114_154232_483911, all 38 loads of `tail_handle_ipv4_cont` failed with `GOT-relative reloc against unknown symbol cilium_subnet_map`. Cilium's loader folds config branches at load (lib/static_data.h:60-62), so the object (3509 insns) becomes 2022 insns and the map ref leaves with the branch -- no `cilium_subnet_map` is created at all (that run creates 182 maps, none this). A static native reference then has nothing to bind. Doing better is a LINKER capability: drop a config branch and the map refs it guards when the captured value folds it. That is PERF's code; lane C records the evidence, does not edit it.

- LANE C 2026-11-16T10:05:00Z: **task (2) — Tetragon `cel_expr_N` (P1j-d19) is written up in `kprog/results/tet-cel-expr-20261116/README.md`, `510066d05`, and it needs your linker, not a producer.** Upstream generates the eight bodies in Go at policy load: `pkg/celbpf/celbpf.go` compiles the CEL expression to `asm.Instructions` and pins the name with `btf.WithFuncMetadata(insns[0].WithSymbol(...), btf.StaticFunc)`; `pkg/selectors/celexpr.go` `functions()` fills all eight slots (compiled body or `CompileEmptyFunction`'s `r0=0;exit`, "otherwise loading will fail due to unresolved symbols") and `RewriteProg` appends them to `prog.Instructions`; `pkg/sensors/tracing/generickprobe.go` keys that on the program name `generic_kprobe_filter_arg`. So the bytes the kernel verifies are the ELF **plus eight bodies that exist only in the tetragon process's memory**. Checked: no object in the image defines any of the eight, and the app object has all eight `U` with one `R_BPF_64_32` per switch case.
- LANE C 2026-11-16T10:05:30Z: **the linker's own rule explains the failure, and the fix is a fallback it already half-has.** `x86_direct_tail_transfers` (`native_lab/native_link/src/x86.rs:466`) resolves a tail branch through the relocation; `resolve_x86_plt32_defined_target_addr` returns `Some` only when the target symbol has a section, and only then does `defined: Some(callee)` get set and receive `PatchKind::DirectBranch`. An undefined target falls to `runtime_call_target` and is treated as a kernel helper address — hence `x86 helper tail branch cel_expr_0 has no helper address`. These are eight application functions misclassified as helper calls, not a missing compiler feature.
- LANE C 2026-11-16T10:06:00Z: **the provider already exists in the shim's capture.** `dump_bytecode` (`bpfopt/shim/shim_state.h:419`) writes the post-rewrite bytecode the kernel verifies, so it contains `cel_expr` *and* all eight generated bodies; `func_info_buf` (`:60-65`) holds the function table — offset plus BTF id, name on `btf.Func`. Two additive steps: (a) persist func_info beside the dumped bytecode; (b) let native-link take `defined: Some(callee)` from that supplementary object for an otherwise-undefined tail target, reusing the existing direct-branch path — no new patch kind, no heuristics. Empty stubs would satisfy the linker but silently replace a real CEL expression, so they are only safe where the slot is empty. Tell me if you would rather own (a) in the shim or want it split; it touches `shim_state.h` only.

- LANE C $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ): **Cilium build facts for PERF** (coordinator 14:20:22Z handed the `cil_from_container` TEST_RUN comparison to PERF; these are the inputs, no run from me).
  - Native objects ship **inside the image** at `/opt/bpf-benchmark/native-bpf/x86_64/cilium/bpf_lxc.native.o`, built by `vendor/bpf/Makefile` (`$(NATIVE_OUT)/cilium/%.native.o`) from `vendor/repos/cilium/bpf` with `CILIUM_MAX_LXC_OPTIONS := $(CILIUM_MAX_BASE_OPTIONS) -DENCAP_IFINDEX=1`.
  - Every `CONFIG(x)` becomes a **global** in `.rodata.config`, and the section is **241 B, 61 symbols, and exactly 2 are nonzero**: `__config_cluster_id_bits = 8` (0x0000) and `__config_device_mtu = 1500` (0x000e). **The other 59 are zero**, including `__config_endpoint_id = 0` (0x000c), `__config_security_label = 0` (0x0018), `__config_kernel_hz = 0` (0x0098), `__config_enable_jiffies = 0` (0x0097), `__config_host_ep_id = 0`, `__config_ephemeral_min = 0`, `__config_enable_ipv4_fragments = 0`.
  - Those zeros are **read at run time, not constant-folded**: `readelf -rW` shows **588 relocations** against the config globals in `bpf_lxc.native.o`, `__config_kernel_hz` 119, `__config_trace_payload_len` 74, `__config_enable_jiffies` 70, `__config_security_label` 46, `__config_endpoint_id` 45. Nothing in `kprog/x86/native_lab/native_link/src/` or `bpfopt/shim/` patches them, and nothing in `kprog/libnativeloader/` does either (grep for `__config_` / `rodata.config` finds only `bpfopt/testccode/native_shims/cilium/lib/static_data.h`).
  - So the native arm runs `kernel_hz = 0` and `LXC_ID = 0` (`#define LXC_ID CONFIG(endpoint_id)`, cf. the parity corpus `bpf.macros:930`), while the eBPF arm runs the agent's endpoint config. That is the most likely source of a per-packet path difference and it is upstream of any TEST_RUN detail. Suggested next step for PERF: dump the map `cilium_lxc`-side identity for the benchmark endpoint in the eBPF arm and compare with the zero the native side reads, then check the jiffies/masquerade branches that `kernel_hz = 0` selects.

- LANE C $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ): **two corrections to my OTel stats-1 entry, both from re-reading the same run's counters.** (1) **`tracepoint__sch` 0.297x is below the repo's `min_runs ≥ 100` filter** — it ran 89 times in the eBPF arm and 96 in the native arm in `195601_628082` (120/93 in `053859_493069`). Where the published row's 1.854x on that program passes the filter only just, this image's 0.297x is 6–10 timings of a single ~4 µs tail and must not be quoted as a ratio; the honest statement is that `tracepoint__sch` has too few invocations to time on this image. (2) **Payload size does not predict per-call cost for `native_tracer_e`, and the split is not a size split.** Today's measured runs, all `status ok`, same original (`tag=1e32ec934f932167`, 3815 B):
  | run | native body | base ns | native ns | native vs eBPF |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `053859_493069` | 10450 B | 3448.6 | 239.6 | **14.40x cheaper** |
  | `125155_179400` | 10477 B | 3189.2 | 3571.9 | 0.89x cheaper |
  | `163206_529710` | 8033 B | 3574.8 | 3746.4 | 0.95x |
  | `184518_934200` | 8033 B | 4256.5 | 5731.1 | 0.74x |
  | `190848_302838` | 8033 B | 4543.1 | 7463.3 | 0.61x |
  | `195601_628082` | 8033 B | 3519.6 | 3667.7 | 0.96x |

  Two bodies of essentially the same size (10450 vs 10477 B) differ by **16x in per-call cost**, and the smaller of the two (8033 B) is uniformly *slower per call than eBPF*. So the 14.4x is not "the linker emitted a big body"; the 10.45 k/10.48 k pair is one body that ran the short path and one that did not. Across the whole history the same size ladder separates cleanly — 7508–7512 and 10450 B bodies sit at 8–20x cheaper, 8033/9248–9445 B bodies at 0.6–1.6x — which points at *which* code path the entry takes at run time, not at emitted bytes. That is A/PERF's OTel-regression question; my measured contribution is that it is per-call and not a throughput artifact.

- LANE PERF 2026-11-16T21:49:07Z: Katran transport source now explains a concrete source of worker imbalance: all 24 retained worker records select queue 0.   **What reproduces on this image: Cilium only** (1.245x / 1.218x against 1.262x / 1.212x, per-call 2.453x against 2.29x). **OTel and BCC fall and the fall is inside the native body**; **Tracee newly completes both modes** (stats 0 clears its floor at 1.179x, stats 1 lands 0.941x just under it, with every high-count program neutral and every regression low-count); Tetragon still fails at the native arm. **Katran's stats-0 decay did not reproduce**: the stats-0 run had the eBPF arm rising while the native arm fell (1.377 / 0.895 / 0.783), but the stats-1 run on the same image has both arms flat and per-call 0.992x — so stats collection is not the cause and the decay is not a property of the image either, just a single-sweep shape that needs one re-run before anyone calls it a regression.

Payload size does not predict it — two ~10.4 k bodies differ 16x — so it is run-time behaviour of the emitted body, not emitted bytes. The unwind counters (`otel-counters-20261116c`, both arms 120 s on this image) close off the alternative explanation: the native arm does **fewer** unwinds (0.888x attempts) and takes the interpreter path **less** often (0.747x), so the workload's path mix improves while per-call cost worsens. That is A/PERF's OTel-regression question, now with a per-call statement and a counter-mix statement that agree.

- LANE C 2026-11-16T23:10:48Z: **Cilium source facts for PERF's next item** (retained, all read from this checkout; no guest used). Summary: **P does not compile the agent's tree, and the live agent's own config values are not all the same as the ones P bakes in, but the three compile-time-hardcoded values P does bake in are exactly the values the agent's own header carries, and none of them is constant-folded in the native body.**
  - *Trees.* P compiles `vendor/bpf/cilium/bpf/*.c` (a tracked **copy**, not the agent's tree; `readlink -f` = `/workspaces/repository/vendor/bpf/cilium`). The agent's own sources are `vendor/repos/cilium/bpf/`. Source divergence is 13 files: `bpf_host.c`, `bpf_lxc.c`, `include/bpf/{access.h,helpers.h,tailcall.h,ctx/skb.h,ctx/xdp.h}`, `include/linux/bpf.h`, `lib/{arp.h,hexdump.h,l2_responder.h,overloadable_skb.h,static_data.h}`. **Correction to my own earlier note**: `node_config.h` and `ep_config.h` are present in P's tree and **byte-identical** to the agent's (`diff` empty for both).
  - *The one semantic divergence in config access is `lib/static_data.h`.* The agent's file is `#ifndef __bpf__ → CONFIG(name) = (__config_##name)`, `#else` an `asm volatile` that reconstructs the rodata pointer on **every** access. P's copy **adds** that same `#ifndef __bpf__` guard to a tree that otherwise had only the asm form. The guard is load-bearing because clang predefines `__bpf__` only on the bpf target: `echo '__bpf__' | clang-18 -target bpf -E -x c -` → `1`, while `clang-18 -E -x c -` → `__bpf__` (literally undefined). So the agent's shipped eBPF keeps the pointer-reconstructing asm form; **P's native x86 body takes the direct-symbol form** and never executes the asm. Same variable, same value, different access shape — the native arm is the only one with a plain `GOTPCRELX` load.
  - *Values P hardcodes at compile time* (exactly three, via `ASSIGN_CONFIG`; everything else is a runtime `NODE_CONFIG`/`DECLARE_CONFIG` left at 0 in `.rodata.config`): `device_mtu` = `MTU` (`lib/nodeport.h:34`), `eth_header_length` = `__ETH_HLEN` (`include/bpf/config/host.h:23`), `cluster_id_bits` = `DEFAULT_CLUSTER_ID_BITS` = 8 (`include/bpf/config/node.h:57`, `#ifndef`-guarded). Measured in the published objects: `bpf_lxc.native.o` `.rodata.config` 241 B with exactly 2 nonzero — offset 0 = 8 (`cluster_id_bits`), offset 14 = 220,5 = **1500** (`device_mtu`); `bpf_host.native.o` 221 B with 3 nonzero — 0 = 8, 4 = **14** (`eth_header_length`), 18 = 1500. The agent's own `vendor/repos/cilium/bpf/node_config.h:110` is `#define MTU 1500`, i.e. **the same value**. `__ETH_HLEN` is 14 and `DEFAULT_CLUSTER_ID_BITS` 8 in both trees.
  - *Not constant-folded.* `volatile const` plus the `ASSIGN_CONFIG` `__check_##name` reference keeps each one a real load: `objdump -dr` shows `mov 0x0(%rip),%rax` with `R_X86_64_REX_GOTPCRELX __config_device_mtu-0x4`. `bpf_lxc.native.o` keeps 588 `__config_*` relocations total, `bpf_host.native.o` more; the top users are `kernel_hz` (119), `trace_payload_len` (74), `enable_jiffies` (70), `security_label` (46), `endpoint_id` (45). So P's native arm is **not** folding config away relative to the JIT'd arm; if anything the JIT'd arm's asm form is the more expensive of the two.
  - *Compile flags.* Per-object, from `vendor/bpf/Makefile:258-264`: `bpf_lxc ← CILIUM_MAX_LXC_OPTIONS`, `bpf_overlay ← MAX_OVERLAY`, `bpf_sock ← MAX_LB`, `bpf_host ← MAX_HOST`, `bpf_wireguard ← MAX_WIREGUARD`, `bpf_xdp ← MAX_XDP`, `bpf_alignchecker ← CILIUM_SIMPLE_OPTIONS`. `CILIUM_MAX_BASE_OPTIONS` (lines 81-92) = `-DSKIP_DEBUG=1 -DENABLE_IPV4=1 -DENABLE_ROUTING=1 -DPOLICY_VERDICT_NOTIFY=1 -DMONITOR_AGGREGATION=3 -DCT_REPORT_FLAGS=0x0002 -DENABLE_HOST_FIREWALL=1 -DENABLE_MASQUERADE_IPV4=1 -DENABLE_NODEPORT=1 -DENABLE_NODEPORT_ACCELERATION=1 -DENABLE_DSR_ICMP_ERRORS=1 -DENABLE_DSR=1 -DENABLE_DSR_BYUSER=1 -DENABLE_HOST_ROUTING=1 -DSERVICE_NO_BACKEND_RESPONSE=1`; `MAX_LXC := BASE + -DENCAP_IFINDEX=1`; `MAX_HOST := (BASE minus -DENABLE_HOST_FIREWALL=1) + -DENCAP_IFINDEX=1`; `MAX_XDP := (BASE minus -DENABLE_NODEPORT_ACCELERATION=1) + LB selection`. Compile rule 266-274: `clang $(CILIUM_NATIVE_OPTIONS) $(NATIVE_CFLAGS) $(core_flags $(CORE_ANSWERS)/cilium.h) -DMICRO_NATIVE_CILIUM -I cilium/bpf -I cilium/bpf/include{,/bpf,/linux} -I cilium/bpf/lib -I/usr/include …`. `NATIVE_CFLAGS` (line 52) is the one common set, `-O3 -g -fPIC -c -fomit-frame-pointer -march=native -mno-red-zone -mgeneral-regs-only -fno-stack-protector -fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables -fno-unwind-tables -Xclang -fembed-bitcode=all`; **no `-D__bpf__`** anywhere, which is what makes the guard above decide the code path. **None of the `ENABLE_*` flags come from the agent's own header** — `vendor/repos/cilium/bpf/node_config.h` has no `ENABLE_*` at all; they exist only in P's Makefile.
  - *What this means for the Cilium item.* The agent-vs-P gap is **not** a config-value gap (the three baked values match, and the ~60 runtime configs are left at zero in both). It is (a) 13 diverged source files including real codegen changes in `ctx/skb.h`/`ctx/xdp.h` (`ctx_data`/`ctx_data_end` and a `__native_skb_net_device` with a `_Static_assert` at ifindex offset 224) and `helpers.h`/`tailcall.h`/`access.h` (`map_array_get_32` native variant), plus (b) the `CONFIG()` access-form change above. If PERF wants one thing to test first, it is whether those native `ctx_data`/`map_array_get_32` variants agree with the JIT'd path on the same packet — that is where a semantic difference could live, not in the config block.

  - The record's GVN diagnosis and my own per-site-IR explanation are both wrong for the optimizer. Routing every literal `RUN_OP` site to its dedicated `X86_SIM_L_EXEC_*` handler (all 2,689 sites map; the 432 helper sites go through `STATIC_HELPER_CALLS`, the same table `direct_step_statement` already uses) cuts frontend IR 18x — 929.3 MB / 198 s at `-O0` → **50.0 MB / 33 s** — and makes `-O1` **2.4x slower**, 356 s → **847 s**. `-ftime-report` on the smaller input has the same shape (InstCombine 494.1 s of 847.1 s). So the cost is optimizer work on surviving code, not emitted bytes; splitting, arm-macro and arm-function rewrites were flat or worse for the same reason.
- The only lever that measured is the level. Since `be3946f6d` compiles B at `-O1`, I checked what that costs the artifact: over all **43** `kprog/x86/micro-prog/*.bpf.c`, `-O1` vs `-O3` gives 9 identical, 28 larger, 6 smaller — **15,253 instructions against 13,624, +12.0%**, worst `packet_record_bounds_window` 453 vs 325 (+39.4%). B is analyzed and never executed (`kprog/docs/design.md:108`), so no timed number moves, but the verifier's input is 12% larger. `be3946f6d` itself proved libbpf *open* for two BCC programs only; the full-size question was closed later and separately — see the last bullet.

- COORDINATOR 2026-11-26T06:21Z: OTel request 14 (kprog/results/otel-request14-20261125, 3c8c3a755), the decision the record leaves to the coordinator: the first shape. load-direct receives the request's captured source (the original object and its loaded instructions), and load_bound_native runs bind_original_source_maps on the checked path as it does on the manifest path. Each ELF map name then binds to the map of the loaded instruction at its relocation site, so perf_progs resolves to the loaded kprobe_progs. Reasons: this is the loader's existing site-by-site composition; the identity is the one the kernel recorded for the loaded program; the kernel's compatibility rule stays as it is. The second shape needs a name or shape match whenever a program holds several prog arrays, and the third changes the kernel. Opened until 2026-11-30 in kprog/docs/freeze-open.tsv: kprog/trusted_loader.cpp, kprog/trusted_service.py, kprog/libnativeloader/src/native_loader_trusted.cpp, kprog/libnativeloader/src/native_loader_source_maps.hpp. omp: this follows the Cilium packet-bounds item now in progress; the evidence is an OTel checked rerun on the rebuilt verified loader with request 14 accepted, and the instruction indices of the original object and the loaded program checked to line up at each relocation site.
